14:T6b1,## Summary

PR #193 introduced grouped org/repo format for lifetime summaries to improve readability for contributors with many PRs across multiple repositories.

This issue proposes extending the same format to day/week/month summaries for consistency.

## Current State (verified from code)

**Lifetime summaries** (new format):
```
PULL REQUESTS:
- Merged: 3 PRs:
  - elizaos/eliza (3 PRs): #6197 "fix markdown spacing" (+10/-5), #6202 "migrate SQL plugin" (+50/-10), #6209 "parallel action" (+200/-50)
```

**Day/week/month summaries** (old format with redundant repo names):
```
PULL REQUESTS:
- Merged: 3 PRs: elizaos/eliza#6197 "fix markdown spacing" (+10/-5 lines), elizaos/eliza#6202 "migrate SQL plugin" (+50/-10 lines), elizaos/eliza#6209 "parallel action" (+200/-50 lines)
```

The repo name `elizaos/eliza` is repeated for every PR. This is verified from `aiContributorSummary.ts` line 153:
```typescript
return `${pr.repository}#${pr.number} ...`;
```

## Proposed Change

Apply the same grouped format to all intervals to eliminate redundancy.

## Benefits

1. **Eliminates redundancy** - Repo name appears once per repo, not per PR
2. **Consistency** - Same format across all summary types  
3. **Clearer repo distribution** - AI can see at a glance where work is concentrated
4. **Token efficiency** - Fewer tokens wasted on repeated org/repo strings

## Implementation

Modify `formatContributorPrompt()` in `src/lib/pipelines/summarize/aiContributorSummary.ts`:
- Remove the `intervalType === "lifetime"` condition
- Apply grouped format unconditionally

## Testing

- Generate summaries for various intervals and contributor activity levels
- Verify AI output quality is maintained or improved15:T5d4,Let's move the private/public icons to the right of the agent name. Method signatures - expected dicts but got Pydantic models
No type enforcement - Python doesn't catch these at compile time
The Rust type system prevents these bugs automatically. The Python fixes we made bring it to parity with the working Rust implementation.

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR fixes the Python chat example and inmemorydb plugin to work together, adds Python quickstart documentation, and improves Character attribute handling. The changes include:

**Key Improvements:**
- Adds comprehensive Python quickstart documentation with examples
- Fixes inmemorydb plugin to use proper Plugin class instead of dict
- Enhances inmemorydb adapter to handle Pydantic models in create_memory/update_memory
- Updates character provider to safely access optional attributes with getattr()
- Adds dotenv support to chat example for .env file loading
- Adds useful helper methods to AgentRuntime (get_available_actions, get_entity alias)
- Enhances get_memories() to accept keyword arguments

**Critical Issues Found:**
1. **Bug in adapter.py line 329**: The update_memory() method references the wrong variable name (`memory` instead of `memory_dict`), which will cause AttributeError when processing Pydantic models
2. **Bug in character.py lines 70-73**: Inconsistent attribute access - uses getattr() in function body but direct access in return data dict, causing AttributeError for optional attributes
3. **Missing dependency in chat.py**: Imports python-dotenv but it's not in requirements.txt
4. **Incomplete documentation**: Quickstart guide doesn't include inmemorydb plugin installation that the chat example now requires

**Impact:**
The bugs in adapter.py and character.py are critical and will cause runtime errors. The missing dependencies will prevent users from running the example successfully.

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- This PR contains critical bugs that will cause runtime failures and prevent the chat example from working
- Score reflects two critical logic errors (wrong variable reference in adapter.py:329 and inconsistent attribute access in character.py:70-73) plus missing dependencies that will cause import errors. These issues will break the example for users and cause AttributeErrors at runtime.
- Pay close attention to plugins/plugin-inmemorydb/python/elizaos_plugin_inmemorydb/adapter.py (line 329 bug), packages/python/elizaos/bootstrap/providers/character.py (lines 70-73 inconsistency), and examples/chat/python/chat.py (missing python-dotenv dependency)

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->1c:T3f51,# Summary

Rust WASM implementation for elizaOS v2.0.0 - enabling the Rust core to run in browser/Node.js environments via WebAssembly.

# Risks

**Low risk.** This PR:
- Makes no breaking API changes to existing Rust code
- Uses conditional compilation (`#[cfg]`) so native builds are unaffected
- All existing tests continue to pass
- Adds new WASM-specific code paths that only activate when building for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR makes the elizaOS Rust core **fully WASM-compatible**, enabling it to run in browsers and Node.js via WebAssembly. The existing Rust crate had WASM bindings but they were stubs - the `WasmAgentRuntime` was a placeholder that didn't actually wrap the real `AgentRuntime`.

The key challenge was that Rust's `async_trait` and many traits require `Send + Sync` bounds for thread-safety, but WASM is single-threaded and doesn't support these traits. This PR implements platform-aware macros and conditional compilation to provide the correct bounds for each target.

### Key Changes

#### 1. Platform-Aware Macros (`src/platform.rs`)

```rust
// Native: #[async_trait] (requires Send)
// WASM:   #[async_trait(?Send)] (no Send requirement)
platform_async_trait! {
    pub trait MyTrait { ... }
}

// Native: pub trait MyTrait: Send + Sync { ... }
// WASM:   pub trait MyTrait { ... }
define_platform_trait! {
    pub trait MyTrait { ... }
}
```

#### 2. Core Trait Migration

All core traits now compile for both targets:
- `ActionHandler`, `ProviderHandler`, `EvaluatorHandler`
- `DatabaseAdapter`, `Service`, `IAgentRuntime`
- Bootstrap traits: `Action`, `Provider`, `Evaluator`, `Service`

#### 3. Real WasmAgentRuntime

The `WasmAgentRuntime` now wraps the actual `AgentRuntime` using WASM-appropriate primitives:

```rust
pub struct WasmAgentRuntime {
    inner: Rc<RefCell<Option<AgentRuntime>>>,  // Not Arc - WASM is single-threaded
    character: RefCell<Character>,
    agent_id: UUID,
}
```

#### 4. Structured WASM Errors

Rich error objects for JavaScript consumers:

```rust
pub struct WasmError {
    pub code: String,      // "VALIDATION_ERROR", "RUNTIME_ERROR", etc.
    pub message: String,   // Human-readable message
    pub source: Option<String>,  // Field/component that caused error
}
```

#### 5. JavaScript Shims

Type-safe wrappers for JS callbacks:

```rust
pub struct JsModelHandler {
    js_object: JsValue,
    handle_func: Function,
}
```

#### 6. Bug Fix: ChannelType Serialization

Fixed `ChannelType` enum to serialize correctly:
- Before: `VoiceDm` → `"VOICEDM"` ❌
- After: `VoiceDm` → `"VOICE_DM"` ✅

## What kind of change is this?

- **Features** (non-breaking change which adds functionality)
- **Bug fixes** (ChannelType serialization)
- **Improvements** (structured errors, better WASM integration)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes require a change to the project documentation:
- Added `examples/README.md` with usage instructions for native and WASM examples
- WASM module includes JSDoc comments for all exports

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `src/platform.rs` - Platform macros (foundation of the approach)
2. `src/types/components.rs` - Core handler traits with conditional compilation
3. `src/wasm/mod.rs` - WasmAgentRuntime implementation
4. `__tests__/wasm/` - TypeScript tests verifying WASM bindings

# Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    Conditional Compilation                   │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]  │  #[cfg(wasm32)]     │
├───────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
│  trait Foo: Send + Sync               │  trait Foo          │
│  #[async_trait]                       │  #[async_trait(?Send)]│
│  Arc<T>                               │  Rc<RefCell<T>>     │
│  tokio runtime                        │  wasm-bindgen-futures│
└───────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     WasmAgentRuntime                         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  inner: Rc<RefCell<Option<AgentRuntime>>>                   │
│                                                              │
│  Methods:                                                    │
│  - create(character_json) → WasmAgentRuntime                │
│  - initialize() → Promise<void>                             │
│  - handleMessage(msg_json) → Promise<response_json>         │
│  - registerModelHandler(type, JsModelHandler)               │
│  - stop()                                                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    JavaScript Usage                          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  import { WasmAgentRuntime, JsModelHandler } from 'elizaos';│
│                                                              │
│  const runtime = WasmAgentRuntime.create(characterJson);    │
│  await runtime.initialize();                                 │
│                                                              │
│  runtime.registerModelHandler('TEXT_LARGE', new JsModelHandler({│
│    handle: async (params) => { /* call LLM */ }             │
│  }));                                                        │
│                                                              │
│  const response = await runtime.handleMessage(messageJson); │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Changes

### New Domain Stores (`src/stores/`)

| Store | Responsibility | Methods |
|-------|----------------|---------|
| `AgentStore` | Agent CRUD operations | get, getAll, create, update, delete, count |
| `MemoryStore` | Memory & embeddings | get, getById, getByIds, create, update, delete, search, count |
| `RoomStore` | Room management | getByIds, getByWorld, create, update, delete |
| `ParticipantStore` | Room participants | getRoomsForEntity, add, remove, getForRoom, isParticipant, userState |
| `EntityStore` | Entity management | getByIds, getForRoom, create, update, delete, getByNames, searchByName |

| `WorldStore` | World management | create, get, getAll, update, delete |
| `TaskStore` | Task management | create, getByParams, getByName, get, update, delete |
| `LogStore` | Logging & summaries | create, getMany, getAgentRunSummaries, delete |
| `MessagingStore` | Servers, channels, messages | createServer, getServers, createChannel, getChannels, createMessage, getMessages, participants |

### Architecture

```
BaseDrizzleAdapter
    │
    ├── initStores()  ───────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                │
    │   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
    │   │           StoreContext                  │◄─┘
    │   │  • getDb()                              │
    │   │  • withRetry()                          │
    │   │  • withIsolationContext()               │
    │   │  • agentId                              │
    │   │  • getEmbeddingDimension()              │
    │   └─────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │                   │
    │         ┌─────────┴─────────┐
    │         ▼                   ▼
    │   ┌──────────┐       ┌──────────┐
    │   │  Store1  │  ...  │  StoreN  │
    │   └──────────┘       └──────────┘
    │
    └── Public methods delegate to stores
```

### Key Design Decisions

1. **Shared Context**: All stores receive a `StoreContext` with database access, retry logic, and RLS isolation

2. **No API Changes**: All public methods remain identical - only internal implementation refactored

- **Lines reduced**: `base.ts` from ~3,900 to ~1,350 lines (~65% reduction)
- **Files added**: 13 new files in `src/stores/`
- **Public API**: No changes
- **Tests**: All 160 unit + all integration tests pass (PGLite + PostgreSQL)

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->20:T1b64,## Summary

Fixes shell environment variable leakage into ElizaOS plugin loading decisions and agent secrets.

**Problem:** `dotenv.config()` does NOT override existing `process.env` values by default. This means shell environment variables (from `.zshrc`/`.bashrc`) leak into plugin loading:

```
User shell has: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY
Project .env has: OPENROUTER_API_KEY

Expected plugins: openrouter, bootstrap
Actual plugins: anthropic, openai, openrouter, bootstrap (LEAKED!)
```

**Solution:**
- Scan installed plugins for declared env vars via `package.json` `agentConfig.pluginParameters`
- Set global filter so only declared vars are captured into agent secrets
- Load `.env` files with proper precedence (closest file wins)

## Changes

- **`packages/cli/src/utils/plugin-env-filter.ts`** - New utility to scan plugins for env var declarations
- **`packages/core/src/secrets.ts`** - Add `setAllowedEnvVars()` filter to restrict captured vars
- **`packages/core/src/utils/environment.ts`** - Add `loadEnvFilesWithPrecedence()` for proper hierarchy
- **`packages/cli/src/commands/start/index.ts`** - Integrate filter before character loading

## Test plan

- [x] Unit tests for plugin env var scanning (`plugin-env-filter.test.ts`)
- [x] Unit tests for secrets filtering (`secrets-filtering.test.ts`)
- [x] Unit tests for env precedence loading (`env-precedence.test.ts`)
- [ ] Manual test with shell env vars vs project .env
- [ ] Test in monorepo context

## Notes

- 40 tests passing
- Backwards compatible (null filter = existing behavior)
- Core env vars (POSTGRES_URL, LOG_LEVEL, etc.) always allowed

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR implements a robust solution to prevent shell environment variable leakage into ElizaOS agent secrets by introducing plugin-based environment variable filtering.

## Key Changes

- **Plugin Environment Scanning**: New `plugin-env-filter.ts` utility scans installed plugins' `package.json` files for declared environment variables via `agentConfig.pluginParameters`
- **Global Filter in Secrets Module**: Added `setAllowedEnvVars()` to restrict which environment variables are captured into agent secrets
- **Environment File Precedence**: New `loadEnvFilesWithPrecedence()` ensures closest `.env` file wins, respecting monorepo boundaries
- **Integration in Start Command**: Filter is applied before character loading, preventing shell vars from leaking into plugin selection

## Architecture

The solution follows a clear flow: scan plugins → set filter → load env files → filter on capture. Core environment variables (e.g., `POSTGRES_URL`, `LOG_LEVEL`) are always allowed. The filter is null by default for backward compatibility.

## Issues Found

- Line 72 in `plugin-env-filter.ts` already correctly checks for null declarations before accessing length
- Line 19 in `secrets.ts` uses underscore variable as TypeScript idiom for unused loop variable (valid pattern)
- Consider caching regex compilation in `detectShellOnlyVars` for performance
- `CORE_ALLOWED_VARS` hardcoded in CLI could be centralized in core package

## Testing

40 tests passing across 3 new test files with comprehensive coverage of filtering, precedence, and integration scenarios.

### Confidence Score: 4/5

- This PR is safe to merge with minor considerations
- The implementation is well-tested with 40 passing tests, addresses a real security concern (shell env leakage), and maintains backward compatibility. Code quality is high with proper error handling and optimization. Minor issues include hardcoded core vars list and potential for regex caching, but these don't affect functionality.
- Pay close attention to `packages/cli/src/utils/plugin-env-filter.ts` for the regex caching optimization and consider centralizing `CORE_ALLOWED_VARS`

    participant Env as Environment Loader
    participant Agent as Agent/Character

    CLI->>Scanner: scanPluginsForEnvDeclarations(cwd)
    Scanner->>Scanner: Scan node_modules for plugins
    Scanner->>Scanner: Read package.json files
    Scanner->>Scanner: Extract pluginParameters
    Scanner-->>CLI: allowedVars Set + plugin info
    
    CLI->>Core: setAllowedEnvVars(allowedVars)
    Note over Core: Global filter set
    
    CLI->>Env: loadEnvFilesWithPrecedence(cwd)
    Env->>Env: findAllEnvFiles (parent dirs)
    Env->>Env: Load files (farthest to closest)
    Env-->>CLI: Loaded env files
    Note over CLI: process.env populated
    
    CLI->>Agent: Load character/agent
    Agent->>Core: setDefaultSecretsFromEnv(character)
    Core->>Core: Filter process.env by allowedVars
    Core->>Agent: Merge filtered vars into secrets
    Note over Agent: Only declared vars captured
```

Updates `drizzle-orm` from 0.41.0 to 0.45.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/releases">drizzle-orm's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>0.45.1</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions breaking in environments with forbidden <code>require()</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/5107">#5107</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>0.45.0</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions</li>
<li>Allowed subqueries in select fields</li>
<li>Updated typo algorythm =&gt; algorithm</li>
<li>Fixed <code>$onUpdate</code> not handling <code>SQL</code> values (fixes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/2388">#2388</a>, tests implemented by <a href="https://github.com/L-Mario564">L-Mario564</a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/pull/2911">#2911</a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>pg</code> mappers not handling <code>Date</code> instances in <code>bun-sql:postgresql</code> driver responses for <code>date</code>, <code>timestamp</code> types (fixes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/4493">#4493</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>0.44.7</h2>
<ul>
<li>fix durable sqlite transaction return value <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/3746">#3746</a> - thanks <a href="https://github.com/joaocstro"><code>@​joaocstro</code></a></li>
</ul>
<h2>0.44.6</h2>
<ul>
<li>feat: add $replicas reference <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/4874">#4874</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>0.44.5</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed invalid usage of <code>.one()</code> in <code>durable-sqlite</code> session</li>

<li>Improved sqlite <code>blob</code> mapping</li>
</ul>
<h2>0.44.4</h2>
<ul>

<ul>
<li>Fixed types of <code>$client</code> for clients created by drizzle function</li>

</ul>
<h2>0.44.2</h2>
<ul>
<li>[BUG]: Fixed type issues with joins with certain variations of <code>tsconfig</code>: <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/4535">#4535</a>, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/4457">#4457</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>0.44.1</h2>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/4586">[BUG]: Drizzle can no longer run on Durable Objects</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>0.44.0</h2>
<h2>Error handling</h2>
<p>Starting from this version, we’ve introduced a new <code>DrizzleQueryError</code> that wraps all errors from database drivers and provides a set of useful information:</p>
<ol>
<li>A proper stack trace to identify which exact <code>Drizzle</code> query failed</li>
<li>The generated SQL string and its parameters</li>
<li>The original stack trace from the driver that caused the DrizzleQueryError</li>
</ol>

<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/releases">drizzle-kit's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.8</h2>
<h3>Bug fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>Fixed <code>algorythm</code> =&gt; <code>algorithm</code> typo.</li>
<li>Fixed external dependencies in build configuration.</li>
</ul>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.6</h2>
<h3>Bug fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/2853">[BUG]: Importing drizzle-kit/api fails in ESM modules</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.5</h2>
<ul>
<li>Add casing support to studio configuration and related functions</li>
</ul>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.4</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed <code>halfvec</code>, <code>bit</code> and <code>sparsevec</code> type generation bug in drizzle-kit</li>
</ul>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.3</h2>
<ul>
<li>Internal changes to Studio context. Added <code>databaseName</code> and <code>packageName</code> properties for Studio</li>
</ul>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.2</h2>
<h3>Bug fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>Fixed relations extraction to not interfere with Drizzle Studio.</li>
</ul>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.1</h2>
<h3>Fixed <code>drizzle-kit pull</code> bugs when using Gel extensions.</h3>
<p>Because Gel extensions create schema names containing <code>::</code> (for example, <code>ext::auth</code>), Drizzle previously handled these names incorrectly. Starting with this release, you can use Gel extensions without any problems. Here’s what you should do:</p>
<ol>
<li>Enable extensions schemas in <code>drizzle.config.ts</code></li>
</ol>
<pre lang="ts"><code>import  { defineConfig } from &quot;drizzle-kit&quot;;
<p>export default defineConfig({
dialect: 'gel',
schemaFilter: ['ext::auth', 'public']
});
</code></pre></p>
<ol start="2">
<li>
<p>Run <code>drizzle-kit pull</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Done!</p>
</li>
</ol>
<h2>drizzle-kit@0.31.0</h2>
<h2>Features and improvements</h2>
<h3>Enum DDL improvements</h3>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>

<summary>Changelog</summary>

<blockquote>
<h1>3.7.4</h1>
<p><a href="https://github.com/prettier/prettier/compare/3.7.3...3.7.4">diff</a></p>
<h4>LWC: Avoid quote around interpolations (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/prettier/prettier/pull/18383">#18383</a> by <a href="https://github.com/kovsu"><code>@​kovsu</code></a>)</h4>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<pre lang="html"><code>&lt;!-- Input --&gt;
&lt;div foo={bar}&gt;   &lt;/div&gt;
<p>&lt;!-- Prettier 3.7.3 (--embedded-language-formatting off) --&gt;
&lt;div foo=&quot;{bar}&quot;&gt;&lt;/div&gt;</p>
<p>&lt;!-- Prettier 3.7.4 (--embedded-language-formatting off) --&gt;
&lt;div foo={bar}&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
</code></pre></p>
<h4>TypeScript: Fix comment inside union type gets duplicated (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/prettier/prettier/pull/18393">#18393</a> by <a href="https://github.com/fisker"><code>@​fisker</code></a>)</h4>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<pre lang="tsx"><code>// Input
type Foo = (/** comment */ a | b) | c;
<p>// Prettier 3.7.3
type Foo = /** comment <em>/ (/</em>* comment */ a | b) | c;</p>
<p>// Prettier 3.7.4
type Foo = /** comment */ (a | b) | c;
</code></pre></p>
<h4>TypeScript: Fix unstable comment print in union type comments (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/prettier/prettier/pull/18395">#18395</a> by <a href="https://github.com/fisker"><code>@​fisker</code></a>)</h4>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<pre lang="tsx"><code>// Input
type X = (A | B) &amp; (
  // comment
  A | B
);
<p>// Prettier 3.7.3 (first format)
type X = (A | B) &amp;
(// comment
A | B);</p>
<p>// Prettier 3.7.3 (second format)
type X = (
| A
&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;
</code></pre></p>
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>

Updates `prettier-plugin-tailwindcss` from 0.6.14 to 0.7.2
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/releases">prettier-plugin-tailwindcss's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v0.7.2</h2>
<h3>Fixed</h3>
<ul>
<li>Load compatible plugins sequentially to work around race conditions in Node.js (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/412">#412</a>)</li>
<li>Fix compatibility with <code>prettier-plugin-svelte</code> when using Prettier v3.7+ (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/418">#418</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v0.7.1</h2>
<h3>Fixed</h3>
<ul>
<li>Match against correct name of dynamic attributes when using regexes (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/410">#410</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v0.7.0</h2>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>Format quotes in <code>@source</code>, <code>@plugin</code>, and <code>@config</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/387">#387</a>)</li>
<li>Sort in function calls in Twig (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/358">#358</a>)</li>
<li>Sort in callable template literals (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/367">#367</a>)</li>
<li>Sort in function calls mixed with property accesses (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/367">#367</a>)</li>
<li>Support regular expression patterns for attributes (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/405">#405</a>)</li>
<li>Support regular expression patterns for function names (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/405">#405</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Changed</h3>
<ul>
<li>Improved monorepo support by loading Tailwind CSS relative to the input file instead of prettier config file (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/386">#386</a>)</li>
<li>Improved monorepo support by loading v3 configs relative to the input file instead of prettier config file (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/386">#386</a>)</li>
<li>Fallback to Tailwind CSS v4 instead of v3 by default (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/390">#390</a>)</li>
<li>Don't augment global Prettier <code>ParserOptions</code> and <code>RequiredOptions</code> types (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/354">#354</a>)</li>
<li>Drop support for <code>prettier-plugin-import-sort</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/385">#385</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Fixed</h3>
<ul>
<li>Handle quote escapes in LESS when sorting <code>@apply</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/392">#392</a>)</li>
<li>Fix whitespace removal inside nested concat and template expressions (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss/pull/396">#396</a>)</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>
<details>

Updates `task-master-ai` from 0.20.0 to 0.40.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/releases">task-master-ai's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>task-master-ai@0.40.1</h2>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1523">#1523</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/fc1a79f2565b0d8c24f009aec2c473a335262ae2"><code>fc1a79f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - Codex cli Validate reasoning effort against model capabilities</p>
<ul>
<li>Add provider-level reasoning effort validation for OpenAI models</li>
<li>Automatically cap unsupported effort levels (e.g., 'xhigh' on gpt-5.1 and gpt-5 becomes 'high')</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1549">#1549</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/98087acae91fad7345bdb4c253d4dfd0d584f81e"><code>98087ac</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - Improve CLI startup speed by 2x</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1545">#1545</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/a0007a3575305c367c8561584aa0dbd181f5e1cc"><code>a0007a3</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - Smarter project root detection with boundary markers</p>
<ul>
<li>Prevents Task Master from incorrectly detecting <code>.taskmaster</code> folders in your home directory when working inside a different project</li>
<li>Now stops at project boundaries (<code>.git</code>, <code>package.json</code>, lock files) instead of searching all the way up to the filesystem root</li>
<li>Adds support for monorepo markers (<code>lerna.json</code>, <code>nx.json</code>, <code>turbo.json</code>) and additional lock files (<code>bun.lockb</code>, <code>deno.lock</code>)</li>
</ul>

You can trigger Dependabot actions by commenting on this PR:
- `@dependabot rebase` will rebase this PR
- `@dependabot recreate` will recreate this PR, overwriting any edits that have been made to it
- `@dependabot merge` will merge this PR after your CI passes on it
- `@dependabot squash and merge` will squash and merge this PR after your CI passes on it
- `@dependabot cancel merge` will cancel a previously requested merge and block automerging
- `@dependabot reopen` will reopen this PR if it is closed
- `@dependabot close` will close this PR and stop Dependabot recreating it. You can achieve the same result by closing it manually
- `@dependabot show <dependency name> ignore conditions` will show all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> major version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's major version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's major version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> minor version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's minor version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's minor version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name>` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency (unless you unignore this specific dependency or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name>` will remove all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name> <ignore condition>` will remove the ignore condition of the specified dependency and ignore conditions

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

## Summary

This PR updates 6 dependencies in the minor-and-patch group via Dependabot:

**Production Dependencies:**
- `drizzle-orm`: 0.41.0 → 0.45.1 (adds new `DrizzleQueryError` error handling)
- `lucide-react`: 0.488.0 → 0.562.0 (adds new icons, renames `fingerprint` → `fingerprint-pattern`)

**Development Dependencies:**
- `drizzle-kit`: 0.30.6 → 0.31.8 (schema generation improvements)
- `prettier`: 3.5.3 → 3.7.4 (TypeScript formatting fixes)
- `prettier-plugin-tailwindcss`: 0.6.14 → 0.7.2 (breaking: dropped import-sort support, changed config loading)
- `task-master-ai`: 0.20.0 → 0.40.1 (major feature additions, 2x faster startup)

## Critical Issue

**The `bun.lock` file is missing from this PR.** This will cause CI failures because GitHub Actions workflows use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, which requires the lockfile to match package.json exactly. The lockfile must be regenerated and committed to this PR.

## Compatibility Analysis

The updates are generally backward compatible with some considerations:

1. **Drizzle ORM**: New error handling is additive and doesn't break existing code. The codebase uses drizzle-orm extensively (37 files) but doesn't use the newly fixed features like `$onUpdate`.

2. **Prettier/Tailwind Plugin**: May cause code reformatting, especially for TypeScript union types and Tailwind class ordering. The prettier-plugin-tailwindcss v0.7.x changes config loading behavior but should work fine with the existing setup.

3. **Lucide Icons**: Potential breaking change if the `fingerprint` icon is used (renamed to `fingerprint-pattern`). Manual verification needed across 34 files.

4. **Task Master**: CLI tool update with no breaking changes to command interface.

### Confidence Score: 2/5

- This PR cannot be merged as-is due to missing lockfile update which will cause CI failures
- Score is 2/5 (critical issues prevent merge) because the bun.lock file was not updated alongside package.json. This will cause immediate CI failures in the pr-checks workflow which uses --frozen-lockfile flag. Additionally, there are potential formatting changes from Prettier updates and a possible breaking change with the renamed lucide-react icon that need verification. Once the lockfile is added, the score would increase to 4/5 as the dependency updates themselves are relatively safe.
- package.json requires a corresponding bun.lock update. After adding the lockfile, verify that no files use the renamed 'fingerprint' icon from lucide-react, and run prettier to catch any formatting changes.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->22:T1832,## Summary

Add **BlockRun plugin** enabling ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using [x402 micropayments](https://x402.org) on Base chain.

- **BLOCKRUN_CHAT** action: Pay-per-request access to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and more
- **BLOCKRUN_WALLET** provider: Wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- Automatic x402 payment handling via EIP-712 signatures
- No API keys required - just a wallet with USDC

## How it works

1. Agent makes an AI request via `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action
2. BlockRun gateway returns `402 Payment Required` with price
3. Plugin signs USDC payment (EIP-712 TransferWithAuthorization)
4. Request retries with payment signature
5. AI response returned to agent

Typical cost: **$0.001-0.01 per request**

## Configuration

```env
BASE_CHAIN_WALLET_KEY=0x...  # Private key with USDC on Base
```

## Usage

```typescript
import { blockrunPlugin } from '@elizaos/plugin-blockrun';

const agent = new Agent({
  plugins: [blockrunPlugin],
});
```

## Test plan

- [x] Unit tests for plugin exports
- [x] Wallet provider returns address and balance
- [x] Action validates wallet configuration
- [x] E2E test with real x402 payments (3 successful API calls)

## Links

- [BlockRun](https://blockrun.ai) - Pay-per-request AI gateway
- [x402 Protocol](https://x402.org) - HTTP 402 micropayment standard
- [@blockrun/llm SDK](https://github.com/BlockRunAI/blockrun-llm-ts)

---

cc @w1kke - discussed integration on Twitter

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR adds a BlockRun plugin that enables ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using the x402 micropayment protocol on Base chain. The plugin provides a `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action for making AI API calls and a `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` provider for wallet context.

## What Changed

The PR introduces a complete plugin package (`@elizaos/plugin-blockrun`) with:
- **Action**: `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` - handles AI requests with automatic x402 payment flow
- **Provider**: `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` - supplies wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- **Dependencies**: Uses `@blockrun/llm` SDK for payment handling and `viem` for blockchain interactions
- **Tests**: Comprehensive unit and E2E tests validating the payment flow

## Architecture

The plugin follows ElizaOS patterns with proper action/provider structure:
1. User sends message to agent
2. Action validates wallet configuration
3. LLMClient makes request to BlockRun gateway
4. Gateway returns 402 Payment Required with price
5. Client signs USDC payment via EIP-712
6. Request retries with payment signature
7. AI response returned to agent

## Key Implementation Details

- **Client Caching**: LLMClient instances are cached per agent ID to avoid recreating connections
- **Wallet Configuration**: Supports `BASE_CHAIN_WALLET_KEY` and `BLOCKRUN_WALLET_KEY` settings
- **Error Handling**: Comprehensive try-catch blocks with structured logging
- **Model Support**: OpenAI, Anthropic, Google models via unified interface

## Critical Issues Found

1. **Hardcoded Path** (test-e2e.ts:9): Import uses absolute local path that breaks for other developers
2. **Missing LICENSE**: Package.json references LICENSE file not included in PR
3. **Git Dependency**: Uses git URL instead of published npm package for @blockrun/llm

## Code Quality

The code is well-structured with:
- Proper TypeScript typing throughout
- Good error handling and logging
- Clear documentation and examples
- Comprehensive test coverage including real payment flows
- Follows ElizaOS plugin architecture patterns

<!-- /greptile_comment -->24:T1e68,## Summary

Optimize `runtime.initialize()` performance through atomic upserts and parallelization of independent operations.

**Results:** Cold start -30%, Warm start -40%

## Problem

The current initialization flow has several inefficiencies:
1. Sequential check-then-insert patterns with race condition potential
2. Multiple round-trips to DB for existence checks before inserts
3. Sequential operations that could run in parallel

## Solution

### 1. Atomic Upserts (plugin-sql/base.ts)

Replace check-then-insert with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`:

```typescript
// Before: Two queries + race condition
const existing = await this.getWorld(world.id);
if (existing) throw new Error('World already exists');
await this.db.insert(worldTable).values(world);

// After: Single atomic query
await this.db.insert(worldTable).values(world).onConflictDoNothing();
```

Applied to:
- `createWorld()` - returns silently if world exists
- `createEntities()` - skips existing entities in batch

### 2. Parallel Initialization (core/runtime.ts)

```typescript
// Before: Sequential
await this.ensureWorldExists();
await this.runMigrations();
const entity = await this.getEntityById(entityId);
const room = await this.getRoomById(roomId);

// After: Parallel where possible
await Promise.all([
  this.ensureWorldExists(),
  options?.skipMigrations ? Promise.resolve() : this.runMigrations()
]);

const [entity, room] = await Promise.all([
  this.getEntityById(entityId),
  this.getRoomById(roomId)
]);
```

### 3. Idempotent Ensure Methods

- `ensureWorldExists()` - Always calls `createWorld()`, DB handles duplicates
- `ensureRoomExists()` - Always calls `createRoom()`, DB handles duplicates
- `ensureEntity()` - New method using upsert pattern

### 4. Code Cleanup (server/message.ts)

Removed defensive try/catch blocks that were handling race conditions no longer possible with atomic upserts.

## Files Changed

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` | Added `onConflictDoNothing()` to createWorld, createEntities |
| `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` | Parallel init, extracted `mergeAgentSettings()`, added `ensureEntity()` |
| `packages/server/src/services/message.ts` | Removed unnecessary try/catch blocks |
| `packages/*/src/__tests__/**` | Updated tests for idempotent behavior |

## Benchmark Details

```
Environment: PGLite (in-memory), 20 iterations each

=== COLD START (fresh DB each time) ===
Delta:  -30%

=== WARM START (agent already exists) ===
Delta:  -40%
```

## Test Plan

- [x] `bun test packages/core/src/__tests__/runtime.test.ts` - 47 pass
- [x] `bun test packages/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/unit/` - 147 pass
- [x] `bun test packages/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/` - All suites pass
- [x] Idempotent behavior verified: calling ensure methods multiple times succeeds

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR successfully optimizes runtime initialization by replacing check-then-insert patterns with atomic upserts and parallelizing independent operations, achieving the claimed 30% cold start and 40% warm start improvements.

## Key Changes

- **Atomic Upserts**: Added `.onConflictDoNothing()` to `createWorld()` and `createEntities()` in `plugin-sql/base.ts`, eliminating race conditions and reducing round-trips
- **Parallelization**: Refactored `runtime.initialize()` to execute world creation and migrations in parallel, then entity/room/participant lookups in parallel
- **Code Cleanup**: Removed defensive try/catch blocks in `message.ts` that were handling race conditions no longer possible with atomic operations
- **Refactoring**: Extracted `mergeAgentSettings()` method and added `ensureEntity()` helper for cleaner code organization

## Issues Found

- **`ensureEntity()` implementation** (runtime.ts:2668): Returns the input entity object rather than fetching the actual persisted entity from the database, which differs from typical ensure patterns and could cause issues if callers expect database-populated fields

## Performance Impact

The changes are well-architected for performance:
- Atomic operations reduce DB round-trips from 2+ queries per entity/world to 1
- Parallel execution of independent operations (world creation + migrations, then entity + room + participants) maximizes throughput
- Idempotent operations enable safe retries without application-level locking

Tests properly verify idempotent behavior and preserve-first-write semantics.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->26:Tf84,## Summary
- Fixes authentication failures in `elizaos agent` commands when connecting to servers with `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` set
- Adds `.env` file loading to CLI agent commands
- Ensures consistency with `elizaos start` command behavior

## Problem
The `elizaos agent` commands were not loading `.env` files, which caused authentication failures when connecting to servers that have `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` configured. The error was: "Unauthorized access attempt: Missing or invalid X-API-KEY from 127.0.0.1"

The `elizaos start` command already loads `.env` files, but agent commands did not, creating inconsistent behavior.

## Solution
Added `.env` file loading to:
- `createApiClientConfig()` function
- `getAuthHeaders()` function

Both functions now call `loadEnvFilesWithPrecedence()` before checking for `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN`, ensuring environment variables from `.env` files are available.

## Changes
- `packages/cli/src/commands/shared/auth-utils.ts`: Added `.env` file loading to authentication functions

Now users can store `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` in their `.env` files and agent commands will automatically use it when connecting to remote servers.

Fixes #5707

<!-- /greptile_comment -->27:T1259,## Summary
- Fixes issue where reflection evaluator fails with "Entity not found" error
- Adds room information lookup before saving facts
- Calls `ensureConnection()` to initialize entity in database

## Problem
The reflection evaluator in `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/evaluators/reflection.ts` was saving facts without ensuring the agent entity exists in the database. This caused "Entity not found" errors when the UPDATE_CONTACT action was triggered.

## Solution
Added room lookup and `ensureConnection()` call before saving facts. If room or world ID is not found, the operation fails gracefully with a warning message.

## Changes
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/evaluators/reflection.ts`: Added room lookup and ensureConnection call
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/__tests__/reflection-entity-connection.test.ts`: Added test for entity connection behavior

<!-- /greptile_comment -->28:T10ed,## Summary
- Fixes issue where action callbacks were not being sent to users in multi-step mode
- Changes empty callback to actually call user's callback with action response
- Ensures action responses (with attachments, etc.) are properly delivered

## Problem
In multi-step mode, the callback passed to `processActions` was an empty function that just returned `[]`. This meant when action handlers called `callback()` with their response (e.g., generated images with attachments), it was never sent to the user. Users would only see "Provider executed: ATTACHMENTS" instead of their actual action response.

## Solution

1. Mark when action callback is called with actual content
2. Call the user's callback with the action's response content

                DB-->>RT: Updated

                RE->>RT: createRelationship({sourceEntityId, targetEntityId, tags, metadata})
                RT->>DB: Insert relationship
                DB-->>RT: Created
            end
        end
        
        RE->>RT: setCache(reflection-last-processed, messageId)
        RT-->>RE: Cache set
    end

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR adds support for configuring the embedding dimension via `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` character setting to skip the ~500ms API call during agent initialization. The implementation simplifies the `ensureEmbeddingDimension` method by checking for a configured dimension first before falling back to the API call.

**Key Changes:**
- Adds `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` setting support in `runtime.ts`
- Refactors `ensureEmbeddingDimension` from 38 to 27 lines
- Adds 4 comprehensive tests covering various scenarios
- Removes duplicate mock setup code in tests

**Critical Issue:**
The implementation is missing validation to ensure the provided dimension is one of the supported values (384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 3072). The database adapter expects these exact values to map to column names (`dim384`, `dim512`, etc.). Invalid dimensions will cause `DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]` to return `undefined`, breaking embedding operations at runtime.

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- Not safe to merge - contains a blocking bug that will break embedding operations with invalid dimension values
- The P0 issue with unsupported dimension values will cause runtime failures when users configure EMBEDDING_DIMENSION to any value outside the supported set. This breaks the core functionality and must be fixed before merging.
- packages/core/src/runtime.ts - needs validation for supported dimension values

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2c:T1852,## Summary

- The watcher was detecting these changes and triggering rebuilds endlessly

## Changes
- **scripts/dev-watch.js**: Added `ignoredFiles` Set to skip watching `version.ts` and `version.js` files
- **packages/cli/src/scripts/generate-version.ts**: Made version generation smart - only rewrites file if version/name/description actually changed
- Removed unused variables (`stdoutData`, `stderrData`, `buildOutput`) from dev-watch.js

## Root Cause
1. `generate-version.ts` writes `BUILD_TIME = '${new Date().toISOString()}'` which changes every build
2. The file watcher sees changes in `cli/src/version.ts` 
3. This triggers a rebuild, which generates a new `version.ts` with a new timestamp
4. Infinite loop

- [x] Touch a source file (e.g., `packages/core/src/runtime.ts`)

> Prevents watch-mode rebuild loops caused by auto-generated version files.

> - `scripts/dev-watch.js`: Ignores auto-generated `version.ts`/`version.js` via `shouldIgnoreFile`, forwards build stdout/stderr directly (removes unused buffers), and logs ignored files; preserves TypeScript-only watching with debounce.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR fixes an infinite rebuild loop in dev-watch mode caused by the auto-generated `version.ts` file changing on every build. The fix involves two approaches:

1. **Smart version generation** (`generate-version.ts`): Only rewrites `version.ts` if version/name/description actually changed, not on every build. This means `BUILD_TIME` becomes stale in dev mode (intentional tradeoff).

2. **Watcher ignore list** (`dev-watch.js`): Added ignore logic to skip watching `version.ts` and `version.js` files.

## Critical Issue Found

The watcher ignore implementation is **too broad** and will incorrectly ignore legitimate files. It uses `path.basename(filename)` to match filenames, which means ANY file named `version.ts` in ANY subdirectory will be ignored, including:
- `packages/server/src/api/system/version.ts` (the version API endpoint)

This breaks hot reload for legitimate source files that happen to be named `version.ts`.

## Root Cause Analysis

The original problem was that `generate-version.ts` writes `BUILD_TIME = '${new Date().toISOString()}'` which changes every build, triggering the watcher. The fix addresses this but introduces a new bug in the ignore logic.

## Additional Changes

- Removed unused variables (`stdoutData`, `stderrData`, `buildOutput`) - good cleanup

### Confidence Score: 2/5

- This PR contains a critical bug that will break hot reload for legitimate files
- Score reflects a critical logic error in the file ignore implementation that will cause the watcher to ignore legitimate source files (packages/server/src/api/system/version.ts). While the smart version generation logic is sound, the overly broad ignore pattern undermines the PR's goal of fixing the development workflow.
- Pay close attention to scripts/dev-watch.js - the shouldIgnoreFile function needs fixing before merge

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| scripts/dev-watch.js | 2/5 | Added file ignore logic to prevent rebuild loops, but the implementation is too broad and will incorrectly ignore legitimate files like packages/server/src/api/system/version.ts. Also removed unused variables (good cleanup). |
| packages/cli/src/scripts/generate-version.ts | 4/5 | Added smart version comparison to prevent unnecessary file writes. Core logic is sound but lacks input sanitization for special characters in package.json fields. BUILD_TIME will become stale in dev mode (intentional tradeoff). |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant DevWatch as dev-watch.js
    participant Builder as Build Process
    participant GenVer as generate-version.ts
    participant FS as File System
    participant Watcher as File Watcher

    Note over DevWatch: Initial Startup
    DevWatch->>Builder: Start CLI build
    Builder->>GenVer: Execute generate-version.ts
    GenVer->>FS: Read package.json
    FS-->>GenVer: version, name, description
    GenVer->>FS: Check existing version.ts
    alt version.ts exists with same version info
        GenVer->>GenVer: Skip write (no change)
        Note over GenVer: Prevents rebuild loop
    else version.ts missing or different
        GenVer->>FS: Write new version.ts
        Note over GenVer: Includes BUILD_TIME (stale in dev)
    end
    Builder-->>DevWatch: Build complete
    DevWatch->>Watcher: Start watching source directories
    
    Note over Watcher: User modifies source file
    Watcher->>Watcher: Detect file change
    Watcher->>Watcher: Check shouldIgnoreFile(filename)
    alt filename basename is "version.ts" or "version.js"
        Watcher->>Watcher: Ignore (too broad - BUG!)
        Note over Watcher: Also ignores api/system/version.ts
    else Other TypeScript file
        Watcher->>DevWatch: Trigger rebuild
        DevWatch->>Builder: Rebuild CLI
        Builder->>GenVer: Execute generate-version.ts
        Note over GenVer: Smart comparison prevents<br/>unnecessary writes
    end
```

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades recharts from version 2.15.2 to 3.6.0, a major version bump that brings new features, bug fixes, and deprecations.

## Key Changes in recharts 3.6.0

**New Features:**
- New `BarStack` component for configuring stacked bar settings
- Support for ranged stacked bars
- New `equidistantPreserveEnd` interval option for XAxis/YAxis
- Additional TypeScript exports (`TooltipIndex`, `BarRectangleItem`, `DataKey`, etc.)
- `graphicalItemId` added to tooltip payload

**Bug Fixes:**
- Fixed Scatter tooltip payload duplication (not applicable - no Scatter usage)
- Fixed Label/LabelList crash
- Numbers are now rounded before DOM insertion
- Improved shadow DOM support with refs instead of IDs

**Deprecations:**
- `CartesianAxis` is deprecated (not used in this codebase)

## Codebase Impact Assessment

The codebase uses recharts in 3 files:
1. **src/components/ui/chart.tsx** - Wrapper component using `ResponsiveContainer`, `Tooltip`, `Legend`
2. **src/components/daily-activity.tsx** - Stacked bar chart with `Bar`, `BarChart`, `CartesianGrid`, `XAxis`, `YAxis`
3. **src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx** - Line chart with `Line`, `LineChart`, `XAxis`

## Potential Concerns

1. **React 19 Compatibility**: This codebase uses React 19.0.0. Recharts v3.6.0 may not have been explicitly tested with React 19, as React 19 is very recent. This could potentially cause peer dependency warnings or runtime issues.

2. **Major Version Bump**: Moving from 2.x to 3.x typically indicates breaking changes. While the release notes provided don't show obvious breaking changes that affect the current usage patterns, there may be subtle behavioral differences.

3. **XAxis interval prop**: The code uses `interval="preserveStartEnd"` in daily-activity.tsx. The release notes mention a new `equidistantPreserveEnd` option, but it's unclear if `preserveStartEnd` is still valid or if its behavior has changed.

4. **Stacked Bar Radius**: The code uses `radius={[0, 0, 0, 0]}` on individual `Bar` components within a stack. The new `BarStack` component allows configuring radius for the entire stack, which might change how radius is interpreted.

## Recommendations

- The PR checks will run build, lint, and typecheck which should catch most TypeScript incompatibilities
- Consider manual testing of the charts to ensure visual consistency
- Monitor for peer dependency warnings during installation
- If issues arise, the new `BarStack` component could be explored as an alternative to the current stacked bar implementation

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR carries moderate risk due to the major version bump and React 19 compatibility uncertainty
- Score of 3 reflects: (1) Major version upgrade (2.x→3.x) which typically includes breaking changes, (2) Limited but clear documentation of what changed in v3.6.0, (3) Potential React 19 compatibility concerns since React 19 is very recent, (4) Usage is limited to basic chart components (Bar, Line, XAxis, YAxis) which reduces risk surface area, (5) PR checks will validate build and typecheck, (6) No deprecated features are used except possibly uncertainty around interval="preserveStartEnd"
- Pay close attention to src/components/daily-activity.tsx and src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx during testing, as these contain the actual chart implementations that may be affected by behavioral changes

File Analysis

|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 3/5 | Major version upgrade of recharts from 2.15.2 to 3.6.0; requires testing for React 19 compatibility and potential breaking changes |

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2e:T1018,This PR adds comprehensive documentation for server environment variables, addressing #5716.

## Summary

Added `docs/environment-variables.md` with detailed documentation for:
- `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` - API authentication for securing endpoints
- `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE` - Web UI control for security and deployment flexibility

## Content

The documentation includes:
- **Purpose and behavior** for each environment variable
- **Default values** and security implications
- **Usage examples** showing how to configure each variable
- **Deployment scenarios**:
  - Production deployment (secure)
  - Development setup (convenient)
  - Headless API server
- **Related files** references for implementation details

## Why This Matters

Proper documentation of these environment variables is important because:
1. **Security**: `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` controls API access - users need to understand how to secure their deployments
2. **Attack Surface**: `ELIZA_UI_ENABLE` allows disabling the UI to reduce exposure in production
3. **Flexibility**: Both variables support different deployment scenarios

Closes #5716

sequenceDiagram

        AuthMiddleware->>Express: "next() - skip auth"
        Express->>AgentServer: "Handle API request"
        AgentServer-->>Express: "Return response"
        Express-->>User: "API response"
    end

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2f:Tc6c,This PR adds the `unregisterAction` method to the `AgentRuntime` class, addressing #5697.

## Summary

Adds the ability to dynamically remove actions from the runtime at runtime.

## Changes

- Added `unregisterAction(actionName: string): boolean` method to `AgentRuntime` class
- Added type definition to `IAgentRuntime` interface
- Added comprehensive test coverage including:
  - Successful unregistration
  - Unregistering non-existent actions
  - Multiple action management

## Method Behavior

The method:
- Searches for an action by name in the actions array
- Removes it if found
- Logs the operation for debugging
- Returns `true` if successful, `false` if action not found

## Use Cases

- Hot-reloading plugin configurations
- Dynamically managing available actions
- Testing and development scenarios

## Testing

All existing tests pass (54 pass, 0 fail).

Closes #5697

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant Runtime as AgentRuntime
    participant Actions as actions[]
    participant Logger

    User->>Runtime: unregisterAction("actionName")
    Runtime->>Actions: findIndex(action.name === "actionName")
    
    alt Action found (index !== -1)
        Actions-->>Runtime: return index
        Runtime->>Actions: splice(index, 1)
        Runtime->>Logger: debug("Action unregistered")
        Runtime-->>User: return true
    else Action not found (index === -1)
        Actions-->>Runtime: return -1
        Runtime->>Logger: warn("Action not found")
        Runtime-->>User: return false
    end
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->30:Tf7c,This PR fixes #5216 - EventType.MESSAGE_SENT event not being emitted when agent responses are sent to the central server API.

## Problem

The `sendAgentResponseToBus` function in `packages/server/src/services/message.ts` sends agent responses to the central server API endpoint (`/api/messaging/submit`), but it does not emit the `EventType.MESSAGE_SENT` event.

This means that plugins with handlers for the `MESSAGE_SENT` event never get triggered when messages are submitted to the central channel.

## Solution

Added `EventType.MESSAGE_SENT` event emission after a successful response from the central server API.

## Changes

1. Added `EventType` import from `@elizaos/core`
2. Emit `MESSAGE_SENT` event with proper payload:
   - `runtime`: The agent runtime instance
   - `content`: The message content being sent
   - `roomId`: The room ID where the message was sent
   - `worldId`: The world ID
   - `userId`: The user ID (agent ID in this case)
   - `agentId`: The agent ID

## Testing

All existing tests pass (532 pass, 0 fail).

Closes #5216

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


Added `EventType.MESSAGE_SENT` event emission after successfully sending agent responses to the central server API. This fixes issue #5216 where plugins with `MESSAGE_SENT` event handlers were not triggered when messages were submitted to the central channel.

**Changes made:**
- Added `EventType` import from `@elizaos/core`
- Emitted `MESSAGE_SENT` event after successful response from central server API in `sendAgentResponseToBus` function

**Issue found:**
- The event payload structure doesn't match the `MessagePayload` interface - it's passing individual fields instead of the required `message: Memory` field. The memory was already created earlier (line 681) and should be retrieved and included in the event payload.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>


- This PR addresses a real bug but has an implementation issue that will cause runtime errors
- The PR correctly identifies the missing event emission and adds it in the right location, but the event payload structure is incorrect and doesn't match the TypeScript interface, which will cause type errors or runtime failures when plugins try to access `payload.message`
- Pay close attention to `packages/server/src/services/message.ts` - the event payload structure needs to be fixed

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/server/src/services/message.ts | Added EventType import and emitted MESSAGE_SENT event, but payload structure doesn't match MessagePayload interface |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant InternalMessageBus
    participant MessageBusService
    participant ElizaOS
    participant Runtime
    participant CentralServer
    participant Plugin

    User->>InternalMessageBus: Send message
    InternalMessageBus->>MessageBusService: new_message event
    MessageBusService->>MessageBusService: handleIncomingMessage()
    MessageBusService->>MessageBusService: Validate subscription & permissions
    MessageBusService->>Runtime: ensureWorldExists()
    MessageBusService->>Runtime: ensureRoomExists()
    MessageBusService->>Runtime: ensureAuthorEntityExists()
    MessageBusService->>ElizaOS: handleMessage()
    ElizaOS->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Runtime: Generate response
    Runtime->>MessageBusService: onResponse callback
    MessageBusService->>Runtime: createMemory()
    Runtime-->>MessageBusService: Memory created
    MessageBusService->>MessageBusService: sendAgentResponseToBus()
    MessageBusService->>CentralServer: POST /api/messaging/submit
    CentralServer-->>MessageBusService: 200 OK
    MessageBusService->>Runtime: emitEvent(MESSAGE_SENT)
    Runtime->>Plugin: Trigger MESSAGE_SENT handlers
    Plugin-->>Runtime: Event handled
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->32:T10ae,<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR enhances the profile summary card by adding markdown rendering support using `react-markdown` and `remark-gfm`. The change replaces plain text rendering with proper markdown formatting, allowing headers, lists, links, and other markdown elements in AI-generated contributor summaries to display correctly.

**What Changed:**
- Added `react-markdown` and `remark-gfm` imports
- Replaced `<p>` element with `<div>` wrapped `<ReactMarkdown>` component
- Added Tailwind Typography prose classes for consistent markdown styling
- Configured prose classes to match the card's design system (custom spacing, colors for headings, dark mode support)

**Integration with Codebase:**
The summary content is AI-generated markdown from the pipeline (see `src/lib/pipelines/summarize/aiContributorSummary.ts`), which produces structured markdown with headers, lists, and links. This change ensures that markdown renders correctly in the profile summary cards, matching the pattern already used in `SummaryContent.tsx` for full summary pages.

**Security:**
`react-markdown` is safe by default - it sanitizes HTML and only renders markdown elements unless explicitly configured with `rehype-raw` plugin (which is not used here). The summary data comes from the database, populated by server-side AI generation, so there's no user-controlled input risk.

**Issue Found:**
The existing truncation logic (line 53) slices at character 300, which can break markdown syntax by cutting through links, headers, or other elements. This needs to be addressed with markdown-aware truncation or an alternative approach.

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR is safe to merge with caution - the markdown rendering is implemented correctly, but the truncation logic will cause rendering issues for long lifetime summaries
- Score of 3 reflects that while the core markdown rendering implementation is correct and secure (proper use of react-markdown with GFM support, appropriate styling), there is a logic bug where the existing character-based truncation will break markdown syntax. This issue only affects lifetime summaries longer than 300 characters when not expanded. The bug won't cause crashes or security issues, but will result in malformed markdown rendering. The positive aspects are: libraries are already in dependencies, security is handled correctly by react-markdown's default sanitization, styling matches the design system, and the pattern is consistent with other markdown rendering in the codebase.
- src/components/summary-card.tsx - specifically the truncation logic at lines 48-54 which needs to be made markdown-aware

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| src/components/summary-card.tsx | 3/5 | Adds markdown rendering to profile summaries but has a critical bug where character-based truncation can break markdown syntax mid-element |

</details>

sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant ProfilePage as Profile Page

    SummaryCard->>User: Display formatted content

    end

<!-- greptile_other_comments_section -->

<!-- /greptile_comment -->33:Ted3,This PR fixes #5707 - an issue where `elizaos agent` commands would fail when connecting to a remote server that uses `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN`.

## Problem

When running `elizaos agent list` (or other agent commands) against a remote server with authentication enabled via `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN`, the command would fail with:

```
WARN: Unauthorized access attempt: Missing or invalid X-API-KEY from 127.0.0.1
```

This happened because the CLI was not loading environment variables from `.env` files before making API requests.

## Solution

Added `loadEnvironment()` call at the beginning of the `getAgents()` function in `packages/cli/src/commands/agent/utils/validation.ts`.

This ensures that:
- `.env` files are loaded before API requests
- `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` is properly read
- All agent commands (list, get, stop, remove, etc.) work with authenticated servers

## Testing

All existing tests pass (13 pass, 0 fail).

Closes #5707

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR fixes authentication issues when running `elizaos agent` commands against a remote server with `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` enabled. The fix adds a `loadEnvironment()` call at the beginning of `getAgents()` to ensure `.env` files are loaded before making API requests.

**Key Changes:**
- Added import for `loadEnvironment` from `../../../utils/get-config`
- Added `await loadEnvironment()` call before creating API client config in `getAgents()`
- This ensures `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` is available in `process.env` when `createApiClientConfig()` reads it

**Impact:**
- All agent commands (`list`, `get`, `stop`, `remove`, etc.) that use `getAgents()` or `resolveAgentId()` will now properly load environment variables
- Fixes unauthorized access errors when connecting to authenticated servers

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk
- The change is minimal, focused, and addresses the root cause of the authentication issue. The `loadEnvironment()` function is already used elsewhere in the codebase and is safe to call. The fix is placed at the right location (before API config creation), and all existing tests pass. The change follows the established pattern of loading environment variables before making API calls.
- No files require special attention

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR extends the Discord plugin with voice/activity features and attachment handling improvements. Key changes include:

- **New DiscordService methods**: `buildMemoryFromMessage`, `setVoiceChannelStatus`, `clearActivity`, `setListeningActivity` enable voice channel status updates and bot activity management
- **Public API exposure**: `buildStandardizedRooms` and `buildStandardizedUsers` methods made public for multi-bot clientRegistry access
- **Enhanced attachment support**: Added base64 data URL and local file path handling in messages.ts for sending images/files from memory
- **Message update utility**: New `update-message.ts` utility and `updateLastMessage()` method enable editing previously sent messages
- **Type safety fixes**: Added null checks before `isDiscordSnowflake()` calls in joinChannel, sendDM, and setVoiceChannelStatus actions
- **Build configuration**: Added `*.d.ts` to .gitignore and improved tsconfig.build.json settings

**Critical security concern**: The new local file path handling (lines 297-301, 340-344 in messages.ts) reads files without path validation, creating a path traversal vulnerability if `media.url` comes from untrusted sources.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>


- PR has path traversal vulnerabilities in attachment handling that need fixing before merge
- Score reflects two critical path traversal issues in messages.ts where local file paths are read without validation (lines 297-301, 340-344). While the rest of the changes are solid improvements with proper null checks and clean implementations, these security vulnerabilities prevent a higher score. The type fixes, new utility functions, and API additions are well-implemented.
- messages.ts requires immediate attention for path traversal vulnerabilities in local file attachment handling

<!-- /greptile_comment -->35:Td49,This PR adds validation to the `ensureDir` function to prevent unclear ENOENT errors when an empty directory path is provided.

## Problem

When `ensureDir` was called with an empty string or whitespace-only path, it would attempt to execute `fs.mkdir('', { recursive: true })`, which results in the cryptic error:

```
ENOENT: no such file or directory, mkdir
```

This error message doesn't clearly indicate that the problem is an empty directory path.

## Solution

Added validation in the `ensureDir` function to check if the path is empty or contains only whitespace before attempting any filesystem operations.

## Changes

- Added validation: `if (!dirPath || dirPath.trim() === '')`
- Throws clear error: `"Directory path cannot be empty"`
- Validation happens before `existsSync` and `mkdir` calls

## Benefits

1. **Clearer error messages**: Users now get a descriptive error instead of ENOENT
2. **Earlier failure**: Fails fast before attempting filesystem operations
3. **Better debugging**: Makes it obvious when configuration results in empty paths

## Related Issues

This fix helps address issues like #5607 where directory creation fails with unclear errors.

## Testing

The validation is straightforward and will throw an error whenever `ensureDir` is called with an empty or whitespace-only string, preventing the ENOENT error from occurring.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


- Adds validation to `ensureDir` function in `packages/cli/src/utils/get-config.ts` to prevent cryptic ENOENT errors when empty or whitespace-only directory paths are provided
- Replaces unclear filesystem error with descriptive "Directory path cannot be empty" message for better debugging experience

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>


| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/cli/src/utils/get-config.ts | Added path validation to prevent ENOENT error when empty directory paths are passed to `ensureDir` function |

<h3>Confidence score: 5/5</h3>


- This PR is extremely safe to merge with minimal risk of causing any production issues
- Score reflects simple validation logic with clear error handling and no breaking changes to existing functionality
- No files require special attention as the change is straightforward input validation

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant CLI as "CLI Application"
    participant ensureDir as "ensureDir Function"
    participant fs as "File System (fs.existsSync/mkdir)"
    participant logger as "Logger"

    CLI->>ensureDir: "Call ensureDir(dirPath)"
    ensureDir->>ensureDir: "Check if (!dirPath || dirPath.trim() === '')"
    alt Empty or whitespace-only path
        ensureDir->>CLI: "Throw Error('Directory path cannot be empty')"
    else Valid path provided
        ensureDir->>fs: "Check existsSync(dirPath)"
        alt Directory doesn't exist
            fs->>ensureDir: "Return false"
            ensureDir->>fs: "Call mkdir(dirPath, { recursive: true })"
            fs->>ensureDir: "Directory created successfully"
            ensureDir->>logger: "Log debug message with created directory info"
            ensureDir->>CLI: "Return successfully"
        else Directory already exists
            fs->>ensureDir: "Return true"
            ensureDir->>CLI: "Return successfully"
        end
    end
```

<h2>task-master-ai@0.42.0</h2>
<h3>Minor Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1533">#1533</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/6c3a92c439d4573ff5046e3d251a4a26d85d0deb"><code>6c3a92c</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/bjcoombs"><code>@​bjcoombs</code></a>! - Add --ready and --blocking filters to list command for identifying parallelizable tasks
<ul>
<li>Add <code>--ready</code> filter to show only tasks with satisfied dependencies (ready to work on)</li>
<li>Add <code>--blocking</code> filter to show only tasks that block other tasks</li>
<li>Combine <code>--ready --blocking</code> to find high-impact tasks (ready AND blocking others)</li>
<li>Add &quot;Blocks&quot; column to task table showing which tasks depend on each task</li>
<li>Blocks field included in JSON output for programmatic access</li>
<li>Add &quot;Ready&quot; column to <code>tags</code> command showing count of ready tasks per tag</li>
<li>Add <code>--ready</code> filter to <code>tags</code> command to show only tags with available work</li>
<li>Excludes deferred/blocked tasks from ready count (only actionable statuses)</li>
<li>Add <code>--all-tags</code> option to list ready tasks across all tags (use with <code>--ready</code>)</li>
<li>Tag column shown as first column when using <code>--all-tags</code> for easy scanning</li>
</ul>
</li>
</ul>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1569">#1569</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/4cfde1c3d54b94701e0fcfc8dbdedbc3bbaf4339"><code>4cfde1c</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/bjcoombs"><code>@​bjcoombs</code></a>! - Improve concurrency safety by adopting modifyJson pattern in file-storage</p>
<ul>
<li>Refactor saveTasks, createTag, deleteTag, renameTag to use modifyJson for atomic read-modify-write operations</li>
<li>This prevents lost updates when multiple processes concurrently modify tasks.json</li>
<li>Complements the cross-process file locking added in PR <a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/issues/1566">#1566</a></li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1566">#1566</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/3cc6174b471fc1ea7f12955095d0d35b4dc5904c"><code>3cc6174</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/bjcoombs"><code>@​bjcoombs</code></a>! - Fix race condition when multiple Claude Code windows write to tasks.json simultaneously</p>
<ul>
<li>Add cross-process file locking to prevent concurrent write collisions</li>
<li>Implement atomic writes using temp file + rename pattern to prevent partial writes</li>
<li>Re-read file inside lock to get current state, preventing lost updates from stale snapshots</li>
<li>Add stale lock detection and automatic cleanup (10-second timeout)</li>
<li>Export <code>withFileLock</code> and <code>withFileLockSync</code> utilities for use by other modules</li>
</ul>
<p>This fix prevents data loss that could occur when multiple Task Master instances (e.g., multiple Claude Code windows) access the same tasks.json file concurrently.</p>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This automated Dependabot PR upgrades `actions/upload-pages-artifact` from v3 to v4, which introduces a potentially breaking change: **dotfiles are now excluded from artifacts by default**.

Key considerations:
- The repository contains a `.nojekyll` file in the root directory that is critical for GitHub Pages deployment (prevents Jekyll from ignoring files with underscores like Next.js's `_next` directory)
- The workflow copies the `out` directory (Next.js build output) but there's no explicit step showing `.nojekyll` being copied into `out/`
- If `.nojekyll` is not included in the artifact, the GitHub Pages site may break
- The v4 release notes explicitly state that if dotfiles are needed, users should create their own artifact rather than using this action

Verify that `.nojekyll` either:
1. Gets copied into the `out/` directory during the Next.js build process, OR
2. Is explicitly copied before the upload step, OR  
3. Is not actually needed (though this would be unusual for Next.js static exports)

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>


- This PR has moderate risk due to the v4 breaking change around dotfile exclusion
- The version bump itself is straightforward, but v4 excludes dotfiles by default which could break GitHub Pages deployment if `.nojekyll` is not properly included in the artifact. This needs verification before merge.
- `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` - verify `.nojekyll` handling before merge

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| .github/workflows/deploy.yml | Version bump from v3 to v4 for `actions/upload-pages-artifact`, no logic changes |

<!-- /greptile_comment -->39:T14af,## Summary

Add BlockRun plugin to the ElizaOS registry.

**Package:** `@blockrun/elizaos-plugin`
**Repository:** https://github.com/BlockRunAI/elizaos-plugin-blockrun
**npm:** https://www.npmjs.com/package/@blockrun/elizaos-plugin

## Description

This plugin enables ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using the [x402 protocol](https://x402.org), paying with USDC micropayments on Base chain. No API keys required - just a wallet with USDC.

**Features:**
- `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action: Pay-per-request access to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and more
- `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` provider: Wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- Automatic x402 payment handling via EIP-712 signatures

**Supported Models:**
- OpenAI: gpt-4o, gpt-4o-mini
- Anthropic: claude-sonnet-4, claude-3.5-haiku
- Google: gemini-2.0-flash

Typical cost: $0.001-0.01 per request

## Links

- [BlockRun](https://blockrun.ai) - Pay-per-request AI gateway
- [x402 Protocol](https://x402.org) - HTTP 402 micropayment standard
